## 2026-09-26 01:48 — codex — T3c early finding: revive crash recovery
Severity: medium (cross-mod data/lifecycle safety). revive/ReviveService.java:575-585, specifically lines 579 and 585. Trigger: player was saved downed before a crash, then logs in while another mod cancels/refuses the lethal LivingDeathEvent/damage. onLogin removes PERSIST_KEY and movement restrictions before calling hurt once; unlike bleedOut at lines 539-545, it does not check whether the player actually died and has no fallback. The player can survive at downed health with no downed marker, so the promised crash/logout death/drop semantics are skipped. Suggested fix: keep the marker until death is confirmed; use the existing bleedOut fallback or otherwise retry safely if the kill is refused, and add a regression test for a refused hit. No edit made. Still reviewing logistics and the other revive edges.

## 2026-09-26 01:49 — codex — T3c complete, read-only
Revive finding 2, severity medium: event/ReviveEvents.java:43-50 calls ReviveService.onDownedDeath from HIGH-priority LivingDeathEvent before ordinary lower-priority listeners may cancel that event. ReviveService.java:519-535 removes the downed record, drag/reviver state, and PERSIST_KEY immediately. Trigger: a downed player being dragged receives a lethal hit that another mod cancels later in LivingDeathEvent. The player remains alive but has lost the downed/drag state; the bleed-out and crash marker no longer protect that transition. Suggested fix: defer irreversible cleanup until death is confirmed (or restore state on cancellation), with a cancellation regression case. This is separate from the previously reported login-after-crash marker issue.
Other reviewed revive edges: pingRevive has one active reviver by UUID and server-side reach/health/dimension checks; EntityTravelToDimensionEvent cancels ordinary travel while downed; damage interrupts active revive on the server; both revive payloads are playToClient only; /hsrevive requires permission 2. ## 2026-09-26 05:29 — codex — T9 early findings: barter trust/exact-once
[P1] Forged duplicate take rows can overflow to a negative price and buy valuable goods for one cheap item. conversation/BarterDeal.java:40 uses takeByIndex.merge(..., Integer::sum); line 46 only checks haveCount < summedCount, so negative aggregates pass, and lines 47-48 create an empty negative-count ItemStack while subtracting a huge value from takenValue. Trigger: take stock index A twice with positive counts Integer.MAX_VALUE and 1 (wraps to -2147483648), also take one real item at another stock index B, and give one dirt. BarterMath.needed(nonpositive takenValue)=0, so the cheap give passes. ListBarterStock.remove ignores the empty phantom row; PeddlerStock.remove sees AIR/count0 for it; both still remove and deliver B. Local 1.21.1 ItemStack source confirms negative count is empty and getCount=0. Arithmetic check confirms the wrap. This is static/data-flow evidence, not an executed in-game exploit. Fix: reject duplicate indexes or sum with checked long arithmetic; require every aggregate >0 and <= actual available count before making stacks or valuing. Apply to both give and take paths, and add a hostile duplicate-row regression.
[P2] Sequential duplicate BARTER_ACCEPT packets execute twice: ConversationService.java:491-514 uses only session.applying, which resets in finally; the same session/stock remains valid after sendBarter(...,1). ConvActionPayload has no offer revision or transaction id. BarterScreen.java:328-335 also does not disable accept pending the response. Trigger: player and stock can cover the same small deal twice; double-click/Enter or replay the same packet before response. Both transactions charge and deliver, relation increases twice; if the first empties a stock row, the second packet's take index can select the next different item. Fix: server-issued offer/stock revision or accepted transaction nonce, consumed before mutation; reject stale/replayed accept. Client pending-disable helps UX but cannot enforce trust. Static control-flow finding; no production edits.
Review of world events and summon continues.

## 2026-09-26 05:33 — codex — T3b early findings
[P1] Dismantling a door refunds it twice. settlement/builder/BuildExecutor.java:360 removes the companion (door upper) with UPDATE_CLIENTS only. In the exact local 1.21.1 Level.setBlock source, flag 2 still performs neighbor shape updates because flag 16 (UPDATE_KNOWN_SHAPE) is absent. DoorBlock.updateShape makes the remaining lower half AIR when upper disappears; Block.updateOrDestroy calls destroyBlock with drops because flag32 is absent. Vanilla oak_door loot drops the lower half's item. BuildExecutor then independently stores the planned door refund (lines357-367). ## 2026-09-26 06:54 — codex — T13 review complete: premature duel yield
[P2] Duel yield uses raw incoming damage before mitigation, so a fully blocked or heavily mitigated hit can decide the raid. ConversationEvents.java:61-63 calls RaidParley.blockDamage from LivingIncomingDamageEvent; RaidParley.java:402-410 compares health minus that amount with the yield threshold and immediately calls endDuel. Exact NeoForge 21.1.248 dependency source confirms LivingIncomingDamageEvent is before damage processing/mitigation (lines 18-22 in net/neoforged/neoforge/event/entity/living/LivingIncomingDamageEvent.java, read from build-codex/moddev/artifacts/neoforge-21.1.248-sources.jar). Trigger: duelist at 8/20 health blocks a 5-damage captain blow with a working shield. 8-5 <= 4 makes the player lose even though the shield should prevent health loss. Armour/Resistance/absorption can cause the same premature result, and captain mitigation can prematurely award a player win. Keep outsider-hit filtering early, but decide yield using effective health damage at the appropriate later stage, accounting for absorption and cancellations, and prevent the lethal hit safely. Add shield-blocked, mitigated-above-threshold and genuine-threshold tests for both sides, with repeated callbacks asserting only one outcome.

Bounded source review of the rest:
- Holding participants have NoAI and their targets/navigation cleared. During a duel the captain is released, while incoming damage filters protect the duelist/captain against third-party attackers and the holding band against ordinary damage. Bypass-invulnerability damage intentionally bypasses the filter. This is damage filtering, not proof of every combat side effect or visual fairness.
- Logout/disconnect, player death, dimension change, leash >20 and 90-second timeout resolve as a player loss on the next server tick. Missing/dead captain, settlement removal or changed raid serial releases the band through tickState. No player-persisted duel lock was found.
- Normal end removes ACTIVE; winning calls resolveRecurringRetreat, whose RecurringRaidRun.resolveRetreat checks active/sealed serial and advances both lastResolvedSerial and lastRewardProcessedSerial before clearing the run. Repeated calls cannot pay/resolve that same normal run again. Loaded raiders are discarded; the remembered-retreated list is used for stragglers. I found no second normal outcome path in this bounded pass.
- Restart does not resume the duel: ACTIVE is in memory, cleared on server stop, and each persisted HOLD_TAG raider is released/unbound on EntityJoinLevelEvent if no active state owns it. Captain has already had hold/binding removed at duel start. This avoids a saved player lock; it does not prove atomic recovery across separately saved entity/settlement/relation data.
- The only current conversation parley GameTest I found is talk_parley_tribute; it checks payment, hold and retreat, not duel shields, player death/logout or restart recovery. Those checks should go through your serialized GameTest lane.

## 2026-09-26 13:01 — codex — T23 civilian-safety review findings
Read-only source review of SettlerPanicGoal, RaiderSettlerTargetGoal, SettlerDefenseTargetGoal, CivilianSafety, RaiderEntity registration and RaidDirector.livingRaidersOf. No production edits, builds or runtime claims.
[P2] SettlerPanicGoal.java:293-322 (also 304-305): rejecting an unsafe destination/path does not cancel the already active navigation. Trigger: a civilian is en route to a house; the band moves onto that side; safeShelter rejects it at the next route cycle. homeTarget/homeBuilding are cleared, but navigation is not stopped. If alternative shelter probes defer or fleeAway fails, the civilian keeps following the previous now-dangerous path until a later successful replacement or timeout. Even with a still-safe destination, routeTo returning false for new raid clearance leaves the previous path active. The fleeTarget fast return likewise keeps its active path without checking the updated band positions. Fix: geometrically revalidate the current path each route cycle; stop it when unsafe before attempting replacement, while retaining a safe path on mere search-budget deferral. Regression: start a safe route, move a raider into its remaining corridor, defer/deny replacement queries, assert the civilian does not continue toward that raider. This preserves BH-31 query caps.
[P2] SettlerDefenseTargetGoal.java:149-156 vs 184-211: the new defender-sighting alarm exists only in start(). Trigger: a Guard is already fighting a zombie; reevaluation selects a visible own-settlement raider (for example it is pursuing a player), and line 208 changes target directly. No start() call occurs and no alarm is raised, so this first defender sighting fails to warn the village. Fix: share the guarded alarm helper between initial acquisition and the successful reevaluation target selection. Regression: start on an ordinary hostile, introduce an own-war raider, force reevaluation, assert settlement alert raised with no duplicate broadcasts while active.
Other requested checks: no unbounded A* loop found in this pass (claimed house <=3 queries, <=2 alternative buildings x3, guard leg <=6; route cycles staggered; shared raid-position query once per settlement/tick). This is source evidence, not a new performance measurement. Trips remain deadline-bounded (240 ticks), shelter timer 600; no demonstrated permanent post-raid flee loop. Null settlement/pendingRaid are guarded. Player targeting remains priority 3 behind settler priority 2, with retaliation priority 1; that retained ordering means defender-distance ranking does not compare players, but the player target goal was not removed. No confirmed new two-shelter ping-pong defect. CivilianSafety is a volatile test seam, not an exposed server config; the class documents that distinction.
T23 source review complete; proceeding to T24.

[P3] LookClientSetup.java:49-51 / LookTextureCache.java:156-166: clear() is wired only to resource reload; no logout/world-unload caller exists. After leaving a world, composed DynamicTextures and decoded NativeImages remain retained until eviction, reload or shutdown. This is bounded retention (320 texture entries +384 layer entries), not an unbounded GPU leak. Add a client logout/world teardown clear on the render thread; check cache empty and textures released after disconnect. Do not clear on server-side LevelEvent.Unload.
[P3] LookTextureCache.java:55,69,86: FAILED is an unbounded set keyed by each distinct look, outside both LRUs. A broken/missing shared layer causes every newly encountered appearance to add another failure string and log entry, retained across worlds until resource reload. Bound negative-cache entries (or cache missing layer failures) and clear on logout. Repro with an absent common layer and many unique appearance keys; assert the negative cache stays bounded.

Costume is SynchedEntityData INT, default 0, saved/read as LookCostume; costumeOf range-checks before array use. Existing looks GameTest covers save/load, but I did not run it or claim network/runtime proof. Texture eviction calls TextureManager.release, decoded-layer eviction closes NativeImage, compose failure closes its output, resource reload clears both. Current get() callers are render/portrait paths and reload clear is the synchronous reload listener; no demonstrated concurrent compositor caller found. Six compositions per 50 ms is a count throttle, not a measured frame-time guarantee. Forty 128x64 composites alone require ~1.25 MiB of pixel storage per CPU/GPU copy, before layers/overhead; the 320-entry composed-image cap is ~10 MiB per copy at that size. This is arithmetic, not measured memory or FPS. Common heroHook defaults false on a dedicated server, so that helper alone cannot prove server-side hero archetype parity; current production voice consumers found are client-side.

## 2026-09-26 13:43 — codex — T31 Captain review findings

[P2] CaptainSpecialGoal.java:108-129,147-157,235-244: hitThisPhase is only cleared when Shield Charge reaches its normal terminal phase. Interrupt the charge after hitting a surviving enemy (stagger/goal cancellation), then charge that same enemy after cooldown: its UUID remains and hitThisPhase.add returns false, suppressing the new charge's damage/knockback. Clear per-activation hit tracking in choose/start or stop, preserving once-per-charge semantics. Regression: hit, interrupt before phase 6, restart after cooldown, same enemy must be hit once again.
[P2] CaptainSpecialGoal.java:133-135 + CaptainService.switchLoadout: an active special's continuation validates hero/stagger only, not changed loadout or re-arm. Switch loadout during a windup: service exchanges the real weapons and sets rearmUntil, but the old special continues and resolves during the 60-tick re-arm period, potentially with the new weapon damage. Fix cancel incompatible in-flight specials on loadout change (spending cooldown consistently), or enforce matching activation loadout and re-arm in continuation. Regression: start old loadout special, switch before contact, assert no old special resolves while re-arming.
[P3] CaptainClient.java:28-29,81 hardcodes DEFAULT_RENDER_SCALE. Server CaptainConfig exposes renderScale but changing it has no visible effect. Sync the configured scale in state or use the synchronized server config; keep it render-only. Current pose scaling does not alter entity dimensions/pathing.
Other checks: promotion is recorded per settlement UUID in overworld CaptainSavedData, markPromoted dirties it and load/save roundtrips the set; no-guard path waits before marking. CaptainState persists readyAt, re-arm, Second Wind and fight clocks, and switchTo does not reset cooldowns. Loadout search reserves both requirements before moving; same-slot reservations are count-aware, source stacks split once, old hand stacks return to player with drop fallback. No confirmed switch duplication/loss in ordinary single-threaded container paths. Special damage goes through hit() -> RoleCombat.isAuthorizedHostile; targeting/area selection also filters enemies. Rename rejects empty result, strips section-sign formatting/ASCII controls and bounds length; it still permits Unicode format characters, a minor display-hardening concern rather than a demonstrated exploit in this pass. Candidate165474641c9fe63409e61bf143a201c42de2865b has invisible clickable building rows, clipped preset text and a cramped/overlapping information pane. Fix BUILDER-UI-ROWS first; these are confirmed native defects. The suggestions below are a usability pass over the existing mechanics.

1. Make the first-use path obvious: Choose building -> Choose design -> Place preview -> Confirm order. One clear primary action per stage. Keep Catalog, Upgrades and Sites as distinct purposes, with a readable active tab and short explanatory line.
2. Building selection: visible names plus small recognisable building icons; selected row clearly highlighted. Show the selected building's purpose and dimensions. Locked entries should explain the actual missing unlock rather than rely on colour. Reuse existing catalog data; avoid an extra filter/search system unless the real list requires it.
3. Give the preview the main space: a large central model, readable design/preset choices with an explicit selected state, and room for full names. Put material information in its own aligned area instead of squeezing preview, costs and lock text together. Show needed/available/missing quantities using the actual authoritative source rules; explain which storage is counted. Never label a command as ready using invented/local stock.
4. World placement: persistent compact instructions for existing rotate/confirm/cancel controls, footprint and orientation. For invalid placement show the actual blocking reason in text as well as colour; retain the ghost so the player can correct the position. Before ordering, clearly show the building/design and real material requirement. Do not add controls that are not implemented.
5. After ordering, show a comprehensible status on Sites: queued, fetching materials, travelling, building, or blocked, only where existing state supports it. Make the current blocking item/reason visible. Preserve the existing queue/priority semantics; no invented completion percentages or new AI behaviour in this UI pass.
6. Give it the character of a medieval building plan: calm wood/parchment frame, a readable drawing with dimensions, strong headings and restrained accents. Use the available space for the selected building and useful information. Match button height, frame and padding with the Staff fixes already requested. Keep destructive actions away from the everyday next step.

Suggested delivery order: (A) restore list labels + remove clipping/overlap; (B) establish selection/preview/material hierarchy; (C) clarify placement instructions and order status. Keep scope to BuilderPlanScreen and existing related presentation owned by the UI lane; request ownership before touching shared components. Please send landed identity for native acceptance at 1280x720 and 1920x1080 with representative GUI scales, long names, locked/available designs, insufficient materials and invalid/valid placement. The end-to-end acceptance is an actual survival player finding a House, selecting a preset, understanding its bill, placing it and identifying what the Builder is doing. No claim of current-tree verification yet.
